This is a custom oscillator designed to give early warning signs and help front-run trades.  It is a B-tier tool — best used in confluence with price action, key levels, and market structure.
Primary timeframe: 3-minute chart. Goal: Identify trend bias, momentum extremes, and high-probability entries/exits.

General Trading Rules: Always start wit

Always start with Trend
Look at the top bar color first.
Look at the orange line for trend direction.
Trade with the trend (retracements) or look for reversals at major structure.

Overextension is Key
When money flow and volume stochastics are overextended → expect reversal or pullback.
Money flow crossing back from overextended zone = high-probability entry area.

Use Confluence
Key levels (order blocks, fibs, swing highs/lows)
3 Drives pattern
Golden Pocket retracements
Market structure (higher lows, lower highs, flags, etc.)

● Design doc shipped to planning/hitl_in_app_design.md (17 sections). BACKLOG P0 entry now links to it.

  Key architectural decisions:
  1. PendingApprovalRegistry — new shared in-process Future registry. TelegramChannel callback handler AND new web POST handler both resolve through it. The Future moves out of TelegramChannel._pending into
  this shared seam. First-decision-wins.
  2. Three new routes: GET /approvals (index), GET /approvals/{order_id} (detail), POST /approvals/{order_id}/decide (action).
  3. LangGraph internals byte-identical to today — interrupt() mechanics, approval_node, TradeFlowState shape, SqliteSaver checkpointer, BoardDecision contract. Phase B only swaps the human-decision channel.
  4. Pair coalescing happens at render time (B.3 / Phase C), not via state-shape extension. Index groups by pmcc_pair_id; detail page auto-coalesces sibling; POST resolves both Futures. Eliminates the §6
  trigger that was killing the prior plan.
  5. Position-context helper extracted to comms/position_context.py returning a structured dict — both the existing Telegram formatter and the new web template render the same data shape.
  6. Audit chain extended with pending_approval_added (registry write) + board_decision_received (registry resolve, source-tagged 'telegram'/'web'/'cli'). Existing board_approved/board_rejected unchanged.
  7. B.1 → B.5 phasing within Phase B for incremental shipping; B.4 is the load-bearing flag flip (Phase A's TELEGRAM_NOTIFICATION_ONLY gets turned ON after a soak period).
  8. Restart recovery deferred to v2 — v1 accepts that an in-process registry is lost on restart (LangGraph checkpointer still holds the suspended thread). Recovery routine reads recent pending_approval_added
  audit rows without matching board_decision_received — Phase B v2 polish.

  Constraints honored (CLAUDE.md):
  - §1 single risk chokepoint preserved
  - §1 audit-before-branch preserved (registry writes audit before resolving Future)
  - §1 paper-default preserved
  - §6 no TradeFlowState change (the killer constraint that drove the redesign)
  - §6 new placement path explicitly documented for Board approval at deploy time

  Open questions deliberately left: SSE vs htmx polling for live updates (v1: polling), CSRF beyond Authelia (v1: skip), CLI channel migration shape (v1: keep working via registry's notifier fan-out),
  auto_execute=true interaction (no design implication — auto orders never appear in registry).

  Files-to-touch list is in the doc § 16. Untouched list is also explicit so future-me knows the boundaries.

  That's a clean stopping point. The Monday-morning scan validates today's PMCC deploys; tomorrow afternoon someone (you or me) reads the design doc and starts B.1.

I will likely use the otter bot indicator but we can discuss that later.


  1. Buy and sell signals — You will receive otter bot and market cipher signals. If I am in cash, you will consider whether to buy or not.  If I own BTC, you will consider whether to sell or not. You determine this not by the indicator signals, the signals trigger you to review the confluence and other factors (price action, volume).  Research best practice confluence factors and add to the strategy.  The goal is that you do not execute a trade without confluence.  Both the otter bot and market cypher have multiple alert types, many of which can be confluence. DO NOT BUY when you get an indicator, watch for confluence and then buy (same for selling). It may make sense to make this part of yaml so it can be easily modified in the future.  Since market cypher is sending signals from the 4-hour and 1-day timeframe, they should be given more weight for higher timeframe trending direction.

  2. Sell discipline.  no hard rule.  you will receive signals and you will confirm these signals with confluence. You will also recognize that of btc is on a 'heater' running up fast, the worst thing we could do would be to sell when bitcoin is just on a quick rest before continuing.  we want to hold bitcoin in this situation.  Since market cypher is sending signals from the 4-hour and 1-day timeframe, they should be given more weight for higher timeframe trending direction.

  3. Gain threshold for a sell to be worth the round-trip.  Do not worry. I am high volume trader and btc spot is basically free.

  5. Existing BTC on Coinbase Spot today. - Count as "already bought," - As we start and stop through debugging, there will be times when I am in cash and in btc.  if we start and I own btc just assume cost basis is the price when sold.

  `web/data.py:build\\\\\\\_donchian\\\\\\\_view` ts_short to read payload.bar_ts
  instead of r["ts"] (~2-line fix; column then matches the bar-open
  time the row's reason text references). Confirm or override before
  writing code.

  After the decision, ship the 3 UI changes on /division/coinbase_spot
  as a single deploy:
    1. Remove Manual Order tile (Donchian + HITL is the surface)
    2. Remove Buying Power tile (cash == buying_power for spot crypto)
    3. 6h price chart with Donchian band overlay + buy/sell fill
       markers + current-bar highlight (Lightweight Charts is already
       a dep — used for the home equity curve). By session start the
       agent will have logged 2-3 more bars (12:02 UTC, 18:02 UTC) so
       there's real data to render against.

  Read order: BACKLOG.md (top section) → memory
  trading_corp_btc_donchian.md → runbooks/deploy_log.md (2026-05-09
  entries cover everything from Phase 2 onward).

  Strategy stays paper-mode (auto_execute: false); every order routes
  through HITL. Real-money risk = zero. The ET conversion shipped a

PROMPT:
Add Polymarket Arbitrage division to Trading Corp
Goal
Add prediction-market trading to the Trading Corp command center as a new division named polymarket, hosting (initially) one strategy: polymarket_arbitrage — LLM-divergence detection comparing market price to a Research-firm-derived true-probability estimate. A second strategy polymarket_copy_trader (mirror top traders by category) is out of scope for this work but the division must be designed so it slots in cleanly later.

Use Polymarket/agents (MIT) as the upstream client library — specifically polymarket.py, gamma.py, objects.py, and the underlying py-clob-client + python-order-utils deps. Do not import or run the upstream repo's LangChain / Chroma / LLM-prompting layers; the Research firm at trading_corp/agents/research/ already does cross-source LLM synthesis.

Read these first (do not skip)
CLAUDE.md — entire file. The §6 "Things to ask before doing" list applies in full.
PROJECT_CONTEXT.md § 11 hard constraints, § 14 glossary.
~/.claude/projects/.../memory/trading_corp_bitunix_vision.md — the phasing pattern this work mirrors.
runbooks/deploy_log.md — confirm nothing Polymarket-related has shipped before writing code.
trading_corp/brokers/bitunix.py — the canonical "new read-only broker, Phase 1" reference.
trading_corp/agents/strategies/coinbase_btc_donchian_agent.py — the canonical "scan-driven strategy on a poll loop" reference.
Settled decisions (do not re-litigate)
Decision	Value
US-ToS posture	Board (Jack) accepts the legal risk; proceed
Wallet	None today — Phase 0 includes "create new Polygon wallet, fund with $500 USDC, store private key in Azure Key Vault"
Polygon RPC	Alchemy free tier; API key in Key Vault
Initial USDC float	$500
Sizing during shakedown	Fixed $1 USDC per trade (paper, then live)
Sizing future	mode: "pct_with_confidence" placeholder reserved in strategies.yaml; not built
Universe	All Polymarket categories
Time horizon	Phase 2: short-tail only (resolves ≤ 7 days). Long-tail deferred.
Aggregate Polymarket exposure cap	$1,000 USDC notional across all open positions
Per-position cap	$1 USDC during shakedown (i.e. fixed sizing dominates)
Investment-type group	New group Polymarkets (its own dashboard tab/section)
OrderSide encoding	buy/sell unchanged; `extra["outcome"]="yes"
Position.symbol shape	f"{market_slug}:{outcome}" for display; condition_id, market_id, outcome_index in Position.extra
Resolution audit kind	Reuse filled with extra.event="resolution", `extra.resolution_outcome="yes"

Branch	polymarket-phase-1 worktree, same workflow as BitUnix
Risk caps (deterministic Python in agents/risk.py)
Cap	Value
Max % division equity per position	5% (forward-compat — fixed $1 sizing dominates today)
Max single-market notional	$250 (forward-compat — fixed $1 sizing dominates today)
Min market 24h volume	$50,000
Max bid-ask spread	3¢
Min time-to-resolution	24 hours
Implied-probability bounds	reject < 5% or > 95%
Daily aggregate Polymarket exposure	25% of division equity, capped at $1,000 USDC
All of these are reads from config/risk.yaml (polymarket.* keys). LLM may narrate verdicts; may not produce them. Tests in tests/test_risk_gates.py.

Phasing
Phase 0 — Wallet + secrets (manual / Board)
Create new Polygon wallet (fresh, dedicated to Polymarket — never reuse a personal wallet).
Fund with $500 USDC on Polygon.

Phase 1 — Read-only broker + division wiring
Definition of done: dashboard renders a Polymarket tile in a new Polymarkets group with real USDC balance + open YES/NO positions. No order path.

trading_corp/brokers/base.py: if the read-only contract isn't already split out, extract ReadOnlyBroker ABC (connect, disconnect, snapshot, quote) and rebase Broker(ReadOnlyBroker) to add place_order + cancel_order. This is the migration TODO in CLAUDE.md "Known sharp edges" — it's load-bearing here because Polymarket Phase 1 must be read-only-by-ABC, not by runtime flag.
trading_corp/brokers/polymarket.py: subclass ReadOnlyBroker. Implement:
connect — instantiate py-clob-client with the wallet + Alchemy RPC.
snapshot — return AccountSnapshot(equity=USDC_balance + open_position_value, buying_power=USDC_balance, cash=USDC_balance, positions=[Position(symbol=f"{market_slug}:{outcome}", qty, avg_price, extra={...})]).
quote(symbol) — parse market_slug:outcome from symbol, return last trade price.
place_order / cancel_order raise NotImplementedError — Phase-1 backstop, exactly like BitunixBroker.
trading_corp/main.py:_build_broker_for_division: new family == "polymarket" branch, mirrors BitUnix branch. PAPER mode wraps with PaperExecutionBroker; LIVE returns the raw broker.
config/divisions.yaml:
- slug: polymarket
  name: Polymarket
  broker: polymarket
  account_filter: main
  intent: aggressive
  benchmark: SPY  # placeholder; revisit
  target_annual_return: 0.50
  standby: true   # UI-only; matches BitUnix Phase 1 posture
  enabled: true
trading_corp/utils/divisions.py: extend classify_investment_type so broker == "polymarket" returns the new Polymarkets group. Update _CRYPTO_BROKERS set NOT to include polymarket.
trading_corp/web/: add the Polymarkets group to the dashboard navigation. Polymarket tile rendering: use the existing snapshot tile shape if it works for binary-outcome positions; if not, extend the rendering path only — do not invent new audit kinds.
Append runbooks/deploy_log.md per the template after deploy. Include **Features shipped:** and **Notable code changes:** lines that future-you can grep for.
Hard rule: Phase 1 ships zero order-placement code. The NotImplementedError is the safety backstop.

Phase 2 — Arbitrage scanner strategy (paper-only)
Definition of done: scanner emits ProposedOrder instances → RiskAgent.evaluate() → would_have_placed audit events with strategy="polymarket_arbitrage" and division="polymarket", visible in the per-division activity rail and in the new "Potential trades" dashboard view.

trading_corp/agents/strategies/polymarket_arbitrage.py modeled on coinbase_btc_donchian_agent.py (scheduler-tick scan-driven shape).
Scanner loop (every 30s):
Pull market list via Polymarket adapter (Phase 1).
Deterministic filter: drop markets failing min-volume / max-spread / min-time-to-resolution / implied-probability-bounds caps (these match the risk caps; pre-filter before risk gate to avoid burning Research-firm calls on impossible candidates).
For each survivor (capped at K per cycle, e.g. K=10): call Research firm's Thesis product asking for an LLM probability estimate on the YES outcome. The Thesis consult is the legitimate cross-source-LLM-synthesis use case per CLAUDE.md §"Research consultation" — but cap the per-cycle call count to bound cost. Do not call Research firm on every market every poll.
Compute divergence = abs(llm_prob - market_prob). If divergence > threshold (config: min_divergence_pct: 10), emit ProposedOrder:
ProposedOrder(
  strategy="polymarket_arbitrage",
  symbol=f"{market_slug}:{outcome}",
  side="buy",
  qty=<USDC notional / share_price>,  # fixed $1 mode: qty = 1.0 / share_price
  extra={
    "outcome": "yes"|"no",            # which outcome share to buy
    "category": <market_category>,
    "market_slug": ...,
    "condition_id": ...,
    "outcome_index": ...,
    "market_id": ...,
    "implied_prob_at_entry": ...,
    "llm_prob_estimate": ...,
    "divergence_pct": ...,
    "resolves_at": ...,
    "max_dollar_risk": ...,
    "tier": ...,
    "source_signal": "llm_divergence",
  },
)
Sizing: config/strategies.yaml: polymarket_arbitrage.sizing:
sizing:
  mode: "fixed_usdc"     # forward-compat: "pct_with_confidence" reserved, not built
  fixed_amount: 1.0
Strategy reads sizing config; never hard-code 1.0.
Persistent state (last-scanned timestamp, per-market cooldown, per-category counters) → agent_state table with staleness gate. Cooldown TTL: 6h per market (don't re-scan a candidate immediately after rejecting it).
Risk gate: extend agents/risk.py with the Polymarket caps from the table above. Tests in tests/test_risk_gates.py.
auto_execute: false in strategies.yaml. HITL approval via web app at trading.jacksumner.com. Telegram = slim deeplink.
Audit: every emission tags strategy="polymarket_arbitrage" + division="polymarket" + category=<...> so per-category activity views work.
Dashboard views (extend trading_corp/web/):
Active trades — filter position table by division="polymarket", render category, current price, entry price, P&L, time-to-resolution.
Potential trades — query audit_event for kind="would_have_placed" AND payload.division="polymarket", render the same shape.
Historical trades — filter position table for closed/resolved Polymarket positions; render outcome, settlement price, realized P&L.
All three views support category filter (drives off extra.category).
Phase 2.5 — Backtester extension
Definition of done: Backtester can model binary-outcome share trades and produce a paper-track approval verdict for polymarket_arbitrage.

Identify whether trading_corp/agents/backtester.py (or wherever the Backtester lives) handles binary-outcome shares today. Almost certainly not — equity / crypto continuous-price models don't generalize to a $0-or-$1 settlement.
Extend Backtester to:
Replay paper would_have_placed events from polymarket_arbitrage over a horizon (suggest 30 days minimum).
For each, look up the market's actual resolution outcome and price path.
Compute realized P&L per trade (binary settlement: bought YES at $X, resolved YES → +$1−X per share; resolved NO → −X per share).
Aggregate: hit rate, avg P&L per trade, P&L per category, max drawdown.
Output a Backtester verdict the Board can sign off on per CLAUDE.md §6 "Backtester approval" rule.
Document in a Board memo following the auto_execute_caps.require_approval_for memo template.
Phase 3 — Live order placement
Only after Board sign-off on the Phase 2.5 memo.

Implement place_order on PolymarketBroker using py-clob-client to sign + submit orders.
Route through graph/ceo_graph.py:build_trade_graph(), NOT a webhook path. Polymarket is scan-driven; the LangGraph path's richer auto_execute_caps is the right home.
Verify auto_execute_caps covers Polymarket-specific triggers before flipping any future auto_execute: true. Likely additions:
require approval if time-to-resolution < 48h
require approval if any single category exceeds N% of Polymarket exposure
require approval if Polymarket equity has drawn down > X% in 7 days
FillEvent.extra carries Polygon tx hash + gas cost (extra.gas_cost_usd).
Resolution poller: separate scheduler tick (e.g. hourly) that walks open Polymarket positions, queries oracle status, and on resolution writes a filled audit with extra.event="resolution" + extra.resolution_outcome + closes the position. Reuses filled rendering on the dashboard.
Phase 4+ (forward-compat, out of scope here)
polymarket_copy_trader strategy in same division: identifies top traders per category, mirrors trades. Separate strategy file, separate risk-cap block, same broker/division wiring.
Long-tail markets (resolves > 7 days). Likely needs different cap structure (lower position size, longer cooldowns).
WebSocket data feed if REST polling proves insufficient.
sizing.mode: "pct_with_confidence" — confidence-weighted sizing tied to divergence_pct and category-specific historical hit rate.
Hard "do not" list
Do not skip Phase 1's read-only-first posture, even if py-clob-client makes live placement easy.
Do not import or run any of the upstream repo's LangChain / Chroma / LLM-prompting layers.
Do not store the Polygon private key in .env on the prod VM. Azure Key Vault, fetched via utils/secrets.py, redacted on root logger.
Do not flip auto_execute: true ever, until: Backtester verdict, Board memo, and Polymarket-specific auto_execute_caps triggers are wired in the LangGraph path.
Do not add columns to proposed_order, audit_event, position, account_state, strategy_state, or agent_state. Use extra_json.
Do not modify the existing TradingView → broker path or the PMCC LangGraph path while doing this work. Polymarket is added in parallel.
Do not call the Research firm in the per-market hot loop — deterministic filter first, then call Thesis on the survivor handful.
Do not hard-code 1.0 as the trade size. Read from strategies.yaml.
Do not ship Phase 2 without polymarket_copy_trader-shaped slot points in the division wiring (so adding it later is "new strategy file" not "rework division").

  Verdict thresholds (Board reads + decides; not a hard gate)

  - n < 30 → INSUFFICIENT_DATA
  - hit ≥ 55% AND avg > 0 AND ROI > 5% → RECOMMEND_APPROVAL
  - hit < 45% OR avg < -$0.05 → RECOMMEND_REJECTION
  - else → MIXED_SIGNAL: continue paper-mode

  What happens when you flip enabled: true

  The strategy starts polling every 30s, fetching ~66 high-volume short-tail markets per cycle, evaluating K=10 with the LLM (~$0.05–$0.20 per call, $2–$50/day). Markets exceeding 10% divergence emit
  would_have_placed audit rows that route through HITL approval on the web app. Over 30+ days the rows accumulate; you run the backtest script; the verdict tells you whether to trust the strategy enough for
  auto_execute: true.

Phase 3.1 shipped — BitUnix is now an autonomous paper-mode trader

  Live on prod since 15:00 UTC. PID 190918 → 192018, clean restart, all 3 observer tables auto-created.

  What's live

  - Volume axis: CVD direction state machine (30 min decay), fed by incoming cvd_bull_flip / cvd_bear_flip webhook events
  - Full tier ladder: PREMIUM (CVD + 4h + 1D agree) / STANDARD (CVD + 4h) / WEAK (no CVD, both HTF agree) / COUNTER (off) / SKIP
  - Order proposer: structural stop = max(1.5×ATR, 0.3%×price), TP at 2R, R:R gate ≥ 1.5
  - Risk caps as the gate: 0.5% effective-risk per trade (downsizes any tier), 3% daily kill-switch (cumulative pre-trade risk)
  - Auto-execute = true in paper mode — no per-trade HITL, just notifications
  - Telegram on placement — every paper trade pings you
  - bitunix_decided audit kind — every signal logs ONE row with outcome (placed / skipped_tier / skipped_daily_kill / rejected_risk / etc.) regardless of trade outcome
  - Multi-leg-ready tp_plan — single leg today, schema ready for Phase 3.2 scale-out (your 25%-at-0.5R / 50%-at-2R / 25%-trailing strategy)

  Verification — synthetic E2E on prod

  - Classified PREMIUM ✓
  - Submitted to risk gate (approved) ✓

  - bitunix_decided outcome=placed ✓

  Test data cleaned post-run.

  audit_event for SQL inspection.

✻ Cogitated for 11m 56s

What went wrong + what's fixed

  The mistake: I anchored on "Coinbase via CCXT is already proven for the Donchian agent" and used that as a default for the new bar cache. When Coinbase rejected 3m granularity, I downgraded to 5m instead of
  switching venues. I framed the venue mismatch as a "Phase 4 follow-up" rather than treating it as the defect it was.

  Why it was wrong:
  - Your historical EDA (data/btc_scalping.db) is BTCUSDT.P from Bybit/BitUnix volatility. Tier thresholds, ATR baselines, all calibrated on that profile.
  - Coinbase spot has different microstructure → real ATR readings would fight the validated thresholds.
  - The tighter principle: trade venue = data venue, no exceptions, especially when the strategy is venue-tuned.

  Now correct:
  LiveBarCache(symbol="BTCUSDT", timeframe="3m", venue="bitunix", max_bars=60)
  - BitUnix's public REST /api/v1/futures/market/kline (no auth needed)
  - Native 3m, no granularity workaround
  - Same venue as execution
  - Verified live on prod: 60 bars cached, last_close=$81,474.90, ATR(14)=$77.34 (~0.095% of price — actual 3m profile)

  Why not Bybit: geo-blocked from US IPs. Both my local dev and the prod Azure VM hit Cloudfront 403. Bybit stays as a CCXT fallback in the code for non-US deploys, but it can't be the live source from this
  infrastructure. (You confirmed this above — TV gets Bybit data because TV's data servers are elsewhere; we trade on BitUnix and our live feed must come from there too.)

  60/60 tests still pass. Deploy log entry written explicitly noting the correction so future me doesn't repeat the mistake.

  Counts:
  - Tier 1 (load-bearing for Phase 3): 36 alerts — 14 Otter 3m + 11 Cypher 4h + 11 Cypher 1D
  - Tier 2 (Cypher 3m for backtest enrichment + future scalp eval): 10 alerts
  - Tier 3 (modifiers + low-conviction signals): 14 alerts
  - Total: 60 alerts on the Bybit BTCUSDT.P chart

  If your TV plan caps you below 60, do Tier 1 first and stage Tier 2/3 over time.

  TV alert message templates

  Otter alerts (use this JSON in the TV alert message body, change signal per row above):

  {
    "strategy": "lord_otter",
    "signal": "otter_buy",
    "ticker": "{{ticker}}",
    "symbol": "{{ticker}}",
    "price": {{close}},
    "time": "{{timenow}}",
    "interval": "{{interval}}"
  }

  Cypher alerts:
  {
    "strategy": "market_cypher",
    "signal": "mc_b_buy_circle_div",
    "ticker": "{{ticker}}",
    "symbol": "{{ticker}}",
    "price": {{close}},
    "time": "{{timenow}}",
    "interval": "{{interval}}"
  }

  Webhook URLs (full, including secret query param if your existing alerts use one):
  - Otter: https://trading.jacksumner.com/webhook/tradingview/lord-otter
  - Cypher: https://trading.jacksumner.com/webhook/tradingview/market-cypher

  (Whatever shared-secret or HMAC mechanism your existing Coinbase Otter/Cypher alerts use — same for Bybit. The handlers don't distinguish by venue, only by signal source.)

  TV interval value mapping (TV substitutes automatically via {{interval}} but if you hardcode):
  - 3-minute chart → interval: "3"
  - 4-hour chart → interval: "240"
  - Daily chart → interval: "1D"

  Multi-asset future — what changes when you add SOL/ETH

  When you eventually add a SOL or ETH chart in TV, you can use the SAME alert templates verbatim — {{ticker}} resolves to whatever the chart's symbol is, so you just duplicate the alert set on each new chart.

  One small code change needed in the BitUnix observer when you start trading SOL/ETH (~5-line fix, do at that point):

  # trading_corp/agents/divisions/bitunix_futures_observer.py
  ALLOWED_SYMBOLS = {
      "BTC/USD", "BTCUSD", "BTCUSDT", "BTCUSDT.P",
      # add when SOL/ETH alerts come online:
      # "SOL/USD", "SOLUSD", "SOLUSDT", "SOLUSDT.P",
      # "ETH/USD", "ETHUSD", "ETHUSDT", "ETHUSDT.P",
  }
  TRADE_SYMBOL = "BTC/USDT.P"  # currently hardcoded — Phase 4 derives from incoming payload

  The bias state machine, CVD state machine, and tier classifier are all already symbol-agnostic — they keyed by (symbol, timeframe) from the start. Only the symbol whitelist and the order's TRADE_SYMBOL need
  updates.

  Per memory trading_corp_bitunix_vision.md: BitUnix scope is {BTC, SOL, ETH} — token whitelist enforced at division entry. So this expansion is in the design from day one.

  What this means for tomorrow's "is anything firing?" check

  If your existing Cypher 4h/1D alerts on Coinbase BTC are ALREADY hitting our webhook (per memory, Cypher webhook handler exists and accepts), then bias state IS being populated and Phase 3 trades may have
  fired overnight. Tomorrow's verification queries will tell us:

  SELECT * FROM bitunix_observer_bias;  -- if this has rows, Cypher alerts are working
  SELECT COUNT(*), tier, outcome FROM (
    SELECT json_extract(payload_json, '$.tier') AS tier,
           json_extract(payload_json, '$.outcome') AS outcome
    FROM audit_event WHERE actor='bitunix_futures' AND kind='bitunix_decided'
  ) GROUP BY tier, outcome;

  If bitunix_observer_bias has rows but bitunix_decided shows 100% skipped_tier, the bias is feeding but tier classifier is rejecting (probably because CVD direction state hasn't accumulated yet — needs ~30
  min of CVD-flip activity to establish direction).

Edit 5 — Add to § 7 "Known sharp edges"
Append:

BitUnix HTF gate ships as mode: shadow (post-PR-3c, branch claude/gallant-tereshkova-49ef85). The gate code paths run on every score-engine fire and write pa_validation_decision + htf_gate_decision audits, but DO NOT block trades or alter sizing. Activates only when YAML flipped to enforce (see § 6 ask-first rule above). Until then, the score engine itself runs the new config (3m/15m/30m TF filter, no PA in score, no guards in score, lower thresholds 5/10/5/3) — net fire-rate change vs pre-PR-3c is unknown until shadow data accumulates.
HTFRegimeConfig.from_dict is not yet implemented (post-PR-3c). The YAML bitunix_futures.htf_regime block IS parsed by yaml.safe_load but main.py uses HTFRegimeConfig.defaults() — so changes to that YAML block don't take effect. Implement before any HTF knob tuning iteration.
Bar archiver is decoupled from LiveBarCache (post-PR-5). LiveBarCache is reused by Coinbase Donchian — don't add BitUnix-specific persistence INSIDE LiveBarCache. The BitUnixBarArchiver is a separate poll loop over a list of caches.
Edit 6 — Optional: add a "BitUnix audit kinds" callout near § 4 "Domain vocabulary"
If you want the new audit kinds documented in CLAUDE.md (vs only in memory), add to the existing AuditEvent definition area:

BitUnix-specific audit kinds (post-PR-3c, 2026-05-14):

pa_validation_decision — every PA gate eval (shadow + enforce). Validators passed/failed + rush_fall trigger + mode.
htf_gate_decision — every HTF gate eval. Full chain: per-TF EMA/ADX/MACD/structure + composite regime + S/R distances + funding + permission outcome + bar_h{1,4,d1}_last_close_ms pointers (PR 5f) into bitunix_bar_history.
htf_regime_snapshot — every 10 min from the new poll loop (PR 5c). Same payload shape as htf_gate_decision minus permission — provides continuous regime time series outside fire moments.

  trade there. This is the expected behavior of your filter.

  Worse problem

  The 2 survivors (Foster: 8,784 lifetime markets; PredMTrader: 3,529) both opted out of position
  visibility on Kalshi. Apify's closed_positions / open_positions calls returned 0 rows for both. The
  profile actor docstring explicitly notes this: "Trade/position visibility is per-user opt-in. Top
  leaderboard names may expose only profile-level data."

  panel on the dashboard renders with 2 rows showing zeros across the board.

  Three ways forward

  1. Manually source correct Kalshi nicknames. Walk kalshi.com and find Domer, GaetenD, etc.'s actual
  handles. Add an alt_handle: retry to the YAML. Probably recovers 4-6 of the 7.

   a DIY scrape of kalshi.com profile pages. Bigger lift.

  didn't make the algorithmic top-12 cut — i.e., observe the "next tier" of refresh_kalshi_whales.py
  candidates. Less interesting socially but tractable.

  What now happens automatically in prod:
  - Every BitUnix score-fire dispatches _build_proposal_v2 → 3-leg tp_plan → paper_trade_record with extra_json.tp_plan_version='v2'
  - paper_trade_replay walks bars → detects tp1/tp2/tp3 fills → updates filled_legs + current_sl in extra_json → emits position_sl_update audits at each lifecycle transition
  - bitunix-position-reconciler (60s) reads the same positions; since replay keeps current_sl aligned with what decide_sl_action would say, reconciler is idempotent in paper mode (no duplicate audits)
  - Dashboard panel populates as audits accrue: Decisions table (entry/SL/tp1/tp2/tp3/sl_method/tp2_method/skip_reason) + SL Lifecycle table (state, current→new SL, filled_legs, source)
  - 24h counters in the panel header track activity rate

  What's now possible that wasn't before:
  - H2 falsification gate (≥30 PREMIUM fires with PREMIUM mean R ≥0.05R better than STANDARD) can finally accumulate — was blocked by 100% PA reject, then unblocked by deferred-fire, NOW also has multi-leg
  paper data so the realized R reflects v2's Option C 0.75R floor instead of single-leg TP3-or-stopout binary.
  - Compare deferred vs immediate fire performance, redeemed vs not, v2 vs legacy, all from paper_trade_record.extra_json queries.

  Phase 4 (live broker placement) is the next gate, still future. Per CLAUDE.md § 5, harmonizing auto_execute_caps between webhook + LangGraph paths is the precondition.

  What you should now see when you re-test:

  1. Demote a PM whale who has open paper positions (like another taylorsversion-style case):
    - Row disappears from Selected Whales (no more lingering on-list-because-of-open-BUYs).
    - Row reappears in Polymarket Watch List with the original leaderboard PnL, win-rate, category
  etc. — NOT zeros.
  2. Demote a Kalshi whale while you're on the WHALES tab:
    - Page reloads.
    - You stay on the WHALES tab (URL fragment now #whales).
    - Demoted whale reappears in Kalshi Watch List with original Apify-scraped stats.
  3. Promote a watch-list whale:
    - Row disappears from Watch List (filtered by selected_whales membership at render time, not by
  deletion).
    - Row appears in Selected Whales with the 📌 badge — zero-stat placeholder until the first
  copy-trade fires.

  Design change worth knowing:
  selected_whales is now the single source of truth for "who we're currently copy-trading."
  watch_only_whales is the immutable observation pool. The watch list panel SHOWS = watch_only_whales
  − selected_whales. This means a previously-demoted whale that was ALGO-selected (sourced from
  leaderboard, never on watch list) will NOT reappear on the watch list post-demote — they had no
  original watch list entry to fall back to. The user-promoted-from-watch-list case is the one that
  fully round-trips.

  Caveats:
  - 261 synthetic SELL audits from yesterday's earlier demotes + however many you added today are
  still in the DB; resolver pairs them as it has been (71/261 paired before, more will pair over
  time). Harmless noise.
  - No commit yet — I'll commit after you confirm the v2 fixes work. Roll-back recipes are in
  runbooks/deploy_log.md for both v1 fallback and full pre-feature rollback.

  Go test it. Tell me what you see.

Phase C complete. 1,102 passed (Phase A 39 + Phase B 21 + Phase C 12 new, all green; same
  pre-existing 12 failures, no new regressions).

  Backtest verdict — 5-factor gate FAILS the pre-committed acceptance bar (3/4 absolute checks PASS,
  fire-rate is the blocker). Report at reports/gate_backtest_2026-05-17.md.

  Headline numbers on the 17-day, 1,796-alert window:

  ┌───────────────┬────────┬──────────────┬─────────────┬────────┐
  │    Metric     │ PA arm │ 5-factor arm │  Threshold  │ Result │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Fires         │ 26     │ 33           │ —           │ —      │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Round-trips   │ 26     │ 33           │ ≥ 20        │ PASS   │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Win rate      │ 50.0%  │ 48.5%        │ ≥ 45.0%     │ PASS   │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Profit factor │ 2.31   │ 2.01         │ ≥ 1.20      │ PASS   │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Total R       │ +14.92 │ +16.16       │ ≥ PA's      │ PASS   │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼────────┤
  │ Fire rate     │ 1.45%  │ 1.84%        │ ∈ [5%, 50%] │ FAIL   │
  └───────────────┴────────┴──────────────┴─────────────┴────────┘

  2×2 cross-tab (1,796 alerts): both fire = 2 · PA-only = 15 · 5f-only = 19 · neither = 1,565. High
  disagreement — the gates disagree on the trades they do take.

  Per-factor pass rates: CVD 63% · VWAP 43% · Volatility 36% · EMA-alignment 17% · Volume-z 16%. CVD
  fallback used 100% of evals (expected for v1).

  What this means:
  - The 5-factor gate is selective and profitable (PF=2.01, WR=48.5%, total R=+16.16) — meaningfully
  better than random on the trades it does fire, and beats PA on total R.
  - It's also tighter than the 5% floor we pre-committed. 1.84% fire rate over 1,796 alerts = ~33
  trades in 17 days, which is below the bar I committed to before running.
  - Holding to the pre-commitment, the report status is FAIL — hold; do not cut over.

  Three options for the Board (your call, not mine):
  1. Accept FAIL → hold and iterate. Loosen one factor — likely candidates: EMA-alignment (17% pass is
   choking the gate) or Volume-z (16%). Re-run.
  2. Re-examine the 5% floor itself. If 5% was actually wrong for a scalping division on
  hostile-regime data, document why and re-commit. (Be honest with yourself — the pre-commitment
  exists to stop exactly this kind of post-hoc move, so this needs a real reason, not just the gate
  looking good.)
  3. Cut over anyway with a memo recording the override. Lowest-friction; loses the discipline of the
  pre-commit gate.

  I'd lean option 1 — loosen the EMA-alignment factor's slope test (currently requires slope > 0;
  could relax to "EMA8 > EMA21 > EMA50" without the slope condition) and re-run. That should bring
  fire rate up to ~5% without giving up much PF, since EMA-alignment is the most-rejecting factor.

  What do you want to do?

  fields. Production's _passes_liquidity gate requires OI ≥ 100, vol ≥ 50, spread ≤ 10%. The helper
  returned open_interest=0 (via int(None or 0)) → instant rejection. A later author added a parallel
  _liquid_call helper and documented the gap at line 1366 ("which is why the pre-existing
  test_pmcc_logic scan tests fail at liquidity") but never updated the shared helper.

  1. Added open_interest=5000, volume=1000.
  2. Tightened bid/ask from mark ± 0.10 to mark ± 0.05 so low-mark weekly fixtures ($1.50) stay inside
   the 10% spread cap.

  actual regression — PMCC scan logic on prod works correctly. Worth keeping the lesson in mind:
  scan-path test failures should be looked at when they happen, not deferred behind a workaround.

  Deliverable 1: cc/trading_corp/agents/ic_telemetry.py — five pure query functions:

  1. combo_pnl_report(strategy, division, start_ts, end_ts, db_url) — groups position rows by extra_json.combo_id, computes per-combo net cashflow via the step-7 signed-qty convention (-qty × avg_price × 100).
   Each combo classified realized / open / partial. Summary aggregates: realized_count, win_count, loss_count, win_rate, mean_win, mean_loss, expectancy, total_realized, open_count. Filterable by strategy +
  division + date range.
  2. win_rate_by_ivr(strategy, start_ts, end_ts, db_url) — pulls ic_lifecycle_closed audit rows; buckets [30-40, 40-50, 50-60, 60-70, 70+]; per bucket: count, win_rate, mean_pnl_dollars, mean_credit_at_entry.
  Unbucketed entries (missing IVR) counted separately.
  3. adjustment_outcome_stats(strategy, start_ts, end_ts, db_url) — splits closed combos by adjustment_count > 0 vs == 0. Per-bucket: count, win/loss split, win_rate, mean_pnl, median_pnl, stdev_pnl,
  total_pnl. Direct test of "did adjustments improve outcomes."
  4. scan_filter_counters(strategy, date_iso, db_url) — reads agent_state.scan_telemetry. Returns by_day (per-day per-symbol per-reason), plus totals_by_reason (cross-day, cross-symbol aggregate). Single-date
  filter supported.
  5. combo_slippage_stats(strategy, division, start_ts, end_ts, db_url) — pulls combo_filled audit rows; distribution of actual_vs_limit_slippage_dollars. Summary: n, mean, median, p90, max, total realized.
  Event list with per-combo detail.

  Deliverable 2: cc/trading_corp/scripts/ic_telemetry_cli.py — argparse with 5 subcommands (pnl / ivr / adjust / scan / slippage). Pretty-printed tables on stdout; --json flag emits raw structures for
  downstream tooling; per-command flags include --start / --end / --date / --limit / --summary-only. Smoke-tested against the real data/trading_corp.db — empty data renders as Realized combos: 0 cleanly.

  ▎ Branch A re-ran the v3 hybrid backtest on the same 17-day window and same 32 gate-pass alerts,

  ▎ median 0.53 bps, p95 1.57 bps, max 11.56 bps). The 1m-Bitunix arm produced PF 1.14 → 1.30, WR
  ▎ 31.2% → 35.5%, SL count 18 → 17. H1 (bar-resolution drove the verdict-collapse from Coinbase PF
  ▎ 2.63 to hybrid PF 1.14) is real but insufficient on its own — the move recovers only ~+0.16 PF and
  ▎  ~+4.3 pp WR against a ~1.3 PF / ~20 pp WR gap to v1.1's Coinbase baseline. H3
  ▎ (granularity-asymmetry — finer bars expose hidden SL touches and push WR down) is explicitly ruled
  ▎  out: WR went up, not down, and SL count fell by one. H2 (cross-venue alert-time price differs
  ▎ between Coinbase and Bitunix-proximate venues, v1.1 was over-fit to v1.0 Coinbase-1m artifacts, or
  ▎  the CVD-fallback proxy used 100% in both arms diverges from real Bybit CVD) survives as the
  ▎ residual explanation. For cutover: PF crosses the pre-committed Phase C 1.20 acceptance bar for
  ▎ the first time on Bitunix-proximate data, but the WR floor (50%) and fire-rate floor (5%) both
  ▎ still fail on this window, so v1.1 stays formally PARKED and live-Bitunix paper data remains the
  ▎ only path to disambiguate H2 from the residual. The cutover memo's pre-data prior shifts from
  ▎ binary (~1.14 OR ~2.63) to a central interval [1.14, 2.63] — paper outcomes near 1.30 read as "H1
  ▎ dominant, H2 residual real," outcomes near 2.63 read as "resolution + native bars + real CVD +
  ▎ alert-source provenance all compounded in v1.1's apparent Coinbase edge," and outcomes ≤1.14
  ▎ reinstate the over-fit interpretation and trigger an architectural-v2 (regime classifier)
  ▎ discussion. Paper data on live Bitunix will naturally resolve the cross-venue alert-time price
  ▎ question, the real-vs-proxy CVD effect, and out-of-regime generalization (this 17-day window was
  ▎ 29/32 hostile-regime sells) — none of which the backtest can answer from the data on hand.
